- **psy-grief-support**: Accompagne le deuil ou une perte significative avec écoute structurée, validation émotionnelle et orientation vers du soutien professionnel quand nécessaire. À utiliser quand l'utilisateur mentionne la perte d'un proche, une rupture, un deuil ou un sentiment de perte profonde. Se déclenche aussi avec "j'ai perdu quelqu'un", "décès", "deuil", "il/elle est mort(e)", "je n'arrive pas à faire mon deuil", "la perte de", ou toute mention de deuil ou de perte significative. Also triggers on "grief support", "I lost someone", "coping with loss". ([khalilbenaz/claude-skills-collection](https://github.com/khalilbenaz/claude-skills-collection/tree/HEAD/skills/psy-grief-support) · ★22)
- **psy-med-side-effect-mood-log**: Suit l'humeur, le sommeil, l'appétit et les effets secondaires possibles d'un traitement psychiatrique sans donner de consigne médicale. Se déclenche aussi avec "depuis que je prends…", "effets secondaires antidépresseur", "mon traitement me fait…", "je dors mal depuis le médicament", ou toute mention de changement d'état sous traitement psychotrope. Also triggers on "psychiatric medication log", "mood and side effects", "treatment follow-up log". ([khalilbenaz/claude-skills-collection](https://github.com/khalilbenaz/claude-skills-collection/tree/HEAD/skills/psy-med-side-effect-mood-log) · ★22)
- **psy-psychiatry-visit-prep**: Prépare un rendez-vous chez le psychiatre avec symptômes, chronologie, traitements et questions prioritaires. Se déclenche aussi avec "rendez-vous psychiatre", "je vois mon psy(chiatre)", "consultation psychiatrique", "préparer mon rdv psy", ou toute mention de visite chez un psychiatre. Also triggers on "prepare for a psychiatrist appointment", "symptoms timeline for psychiatry", "medication questions". ([khalilbenaz/claude-skills-collection](https://github.com/khalilbenaz/claude-skills-collection/tree/HEAD/skills/psy-psychiatry-visit-prep) · ★22)
- **psy-psychology-visit-prep**: Prépare un premier rendez-vous ou une séance chez le psychologue avec objectifs, difficultés et thèmes à aborder. Se déclenche aussi avec "rendez-vous psychologue", "première séance psy", "je commence une thérapie", "je ne sais pas quoi dire au psy", ou toute préparation de consultation psychologique. Also triggers on "prepare for therapy session", "first session with a psychologist", "what to bring up in therapy". ([khalilbenaz/claude-skills-collection](https://github.com/khalilbenaz/claude-skills-collection/tree/HEAD/skills/psy-psychology-visit-prep) · ★22)
- **psy-therapy-journal**: Transforme une expérience émotionnelle ou relationnelle en journal structuré utile pour une thérapie ou un suivi personnel. Se déclenche aussi avec "j'ai vécu quelque chose de difficile", "je veux écrire ce que je ressens", "journal thérapie", "préparer ma séance", ou quand l'utilisateur raconte une situation émotionnellement chargée. Also triggers on "therapy journal", "journal an emotional experience", "notes for my therapist". ([khalilbenaz/claude-skills-collection](https://github.com/khalilbenaz/claude-skills-collection/tree/HEAD/skills/psy-therapy-journal) · ★22)
- **psych-accessibility**: Accessibility and inclusivity validation for game development. Invoked by game-psychologist agent at validation checkpoints. Applies UDL, WCAG, Sensory Processing Theory, neurodiversity considerations, and cultural sensitivity to validate inclusive design. ([Hexcelerate/web-game-dev](https://github.com/Hexcelerate/web-game-dev/tree/HEAD/skills/psych-accessibility))
- **psych-behavioral**: Behavioral design validation for game development. Invoked by game-psychologist agent at validation checkpoints. Applies Fogg Behavior Model, Hook Model (Nir Eyal), Nudge Theory, Habit Formation, and Session Design to validate behavioral patterns. ([Hexcelerate/web-game-dev](https://github.com/Hexcelerate/web-game-dev/tree/HEAD/skills/psych-behavioral))
- **psych-development-personality-social-clinical-and-neurodiversity**: Use when the question concerns individuals and groups: development across the lifespan, personality and the trait models and their predictive validity, motivation, social psychology including the classic studies and which of them survived scrutiny, clinical psychology and what the outcome evidence says actually works, intelligence and what the measures do and do not capture, and neurodiversity. Reports the evidence and its quality; not clinical advice or a basis for diagnosis. ([the-vibey-project/vibey](https://github.com/the-vibey-project/vibey/tree/HEAD/src/vibey_tools/skills/plugins/psychology-sociology-cultural-sciences/skills/psych-development-personality-social-clinical-and-neurodiversity) · ★2)
- **psych-developmental**: Developmental psychology validation for game development. Invoked by game-psychologist agent at validation checkpoints. Applies Erikson's Stages, Gardner's Multiple Intelligences, Executive Function Development, and Piaget to validate age-appropriateness. ([Hexcelerate/web-game-dev](https://github.com/Hexcelerate/web-game-dev/tree/HEAD/skills/psych-developmental))
- **psych-epistemic-situation-and-replication-reform**: Use before relying on any finding from these fields: what the replication crisis actually established and the genuinely encouraging finding alongside it, the reliability gradient as the practical takeaway, how to read a claim in these fields, why the fields are methodologically hard, and what the reform movement changed in practice. Includes the router for the whole psychology-sociology-cultural-sciences reference. ([the-vibey-project/vibey](https://github.com/the-vibey-project/vibey/tree/HEAD/src/vibey_tools/skills/plugins/psychology-sociology-cultural-sciences/skills/psych-epistemic-situation-and-replication-reform) · ★2)
- **psych-learning**: Cognitive and learning science validation for game development. Invoked by game-psychologist agent at validation checkpoints. Applies Bloom's Taxonomy, Piaget, Vygotsky ZPD, Dual Coding, Multimedia Learning, and Spaced Repetition to validate learning effectiveness. ([Hexcelerate/web-game-dev](https://github.com/Hexcelerate/web-game-dev/tree/HEAD/skills/psych-learning))
- **psych-motivation**: Motivation and engagement validation for game development. Invoked by game-psychologist agent at validation checkpoints. Applies Self-Determination Theory, Flow Theory, Expectancy-Value, Attribution Theory, and Operant Conditioning to validate engagement design. ([Hexcelerate/web-game-dev](https://github.com/Hexcelerate/web-game-dev/tree/HEAD/skills/psych-motivation))
- **psych-perception-memory-learning-cognition-and-emotion**: Use when working with the well-established core of cognitive psychology: perception and attention including change blindness and attentional limits, memory and its systems, reconstruction and the reliability of eyewitness recall, learning and conditioning, cognition, reasoning and the biases that replicated versus those that did not, and emotion and the competing theories of what an emotion is. ([the-vibey-project/vibey](https://github.com/the-vibey-project/vibey/tree/HEAD/src/vibey_tools/skills/plugins/psychology-sociology-cultural-sciences/skills/psych-perception-memory-learning-cognition-and-emotion) · ★2)
- **psych-reference**: Use when checking what is live (where replication reform actually stands, and the social-media-and-adolescent-mental-health dispute presented as a genuine dispute rather than resolved, verified August 2026), correcting a behavioural-science misconception, finding the canon, or needing a picker and the red flags to check in any behavioural-science claim. Companion to the other psychology-sociology-cultural-sciences skills. ([the-vibey-project/vibey](https://github.com/the-vibey-project/vibey/tree/HEAD/src/vibey_tools/skills/plugins/psychology-sociology-cultural-sciences/skills/psych-reference) · ★2)
- **psych-sociology-institutions-culture-and-the-weird-problem**: Use when the unit of analysis is social rather than individual: sociological thinking and what it adds, the theory traditions and their disagreements, stratification and social mobility, network structure and its consequences, institutions and organizations, deviance and social control, religion and the secularization debate, culture and anthropology and the ethnographic method, and the WEIRD problem — why a discipline built on unrepresentative samples generalizes badly. ([the-vibey-project/vibey](https://github.com/the-vibey-project/vibey/tree/HEAD/src/vibey_tools/skills/plugins/psychology-sociology-cultural-sciences/skills/psych-sociology-institutions-culture-and-the-weird-problem) · ★2)
- **psychbull-inclusion-and-coding**: Use when defining eligibility criteria and coding studies for a Psychological Bulletin review or meta-analysis — inclusion/exclusion rules, a codebook, double-coding, and inter-rater reliability. Governs how studies enter and are coded; effect-size modeling lives in psychbull-meta-analysis-methods. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-inclusion-and-coding) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-literature-search-strategy**: Use when designing and documenting the systematic literature search for a Psychological Bulletin review or meta-analysis — databases, search strings, grey literature, deduplication, and a PRISMA-compliant records flow. Builds a reproducible search; it does not screen or code studies. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-literature-search-strategy) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-meta-analysis-methods**: Use when computing effect sizes and fitting the meta-analytic model for a Psychological Bulletin manuscript — effect-size metrics, random-effects vs. fixed-effect choice, dependent effect sizes (RVE / multilevel), and heterogeneity (Q, I², τ², prediction interval). Guides the core synthesis; moderators and bias diagnostics live in psychbull-moderators-and-bias. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-meta-analysis-methods) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-moderators-and-bias**: Use when explaining heterogeneity and probing robustness in a Psychological Bulletin meta-analysis — moderator/subgroup analysis, meta-regression, and publication-bias diagnostics (funnel, Egger, trim-and-fill, PET-PEESE, p-curve, selection models) plus sensitivity analyses. Extends the core model; estimation lives in psychbull-meta-analysis-methods. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-moderators-and-bias) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-open-science-and-transparency**: Use when planning and assembling the transparency package for a Psychological Bulletin submission — protocol preregistration (PROSPERO/OSF), TOP-compliant disclosure, and depositing the database, codebook, and analysis scripts. Covers transparency requirements; it does not run the analysis. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-open-science-and-transparency) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-rebuttal**: Use when writing the response to a Psychological Bulletin revise-and-resubmit. A synthesis R&R often demands re-running the search, adding studies, or new bias/moderator analyses, so the response must satisfy every reviewer and the editor while keeping the meta-analysis internally consistent. Structures the response letter; it does not fabricate results. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-rebuttal) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-submission**: Use when running the final pre-submission preflight for Psychological Bulletin via APA Editorial Manager — masking, the ≤ 250-word abstract, APA 7th style, MARS/PRISMA/JARS checklists, and the transparency package. Final checks; it does not draft content. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-submission) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-tables-figures**: Use when building exhibits for a Psychological Bulletin manuscript — the PRISMA flow diagram, forest plots, funnel/bias plots, moderator bubble plots, and MARS-ready summary tables in APA 7th-edition format. Guides exhibit design; it does not generate the underlying estimates. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-tables-figures) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-theory-integration**: Use when turning the synthesis results of a Psychological Bulletin manuscript into a genuine theoretical contribution — reconciling conflicting findings, building or refining a model, and stating scope conditions and a research agenda. Makes the synthesis matter; it does not run the analysis. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-theory-integration) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-topic-selection**: Use when deciding whether a question is review-worthy and meta-analyzable for Psychological Bulletin and which synthesis type fits (meta-analysis, systematic review, meta-review/meta-synthesis, qualitative review). Tests fit for an APA research-synthesis flagship; it does not write the review. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-topic-selection) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-workflow**: Use as the entry point for any Psychological Bulletin manuscript. Routes to the right psychbull sub-skill based on where you are in the research-synthesis lifecycle and which synthesis type (meta-analysis, systematic review, meta-review/meta-synthesis, qualitative review) fits. Psychological Bulletin publishes syntheses, not original studies — it dispatches; it does not draft content. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-workflow) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-writing-style**: Use when drafting and polishing a Psychological Bulletin manuscript so it reads as an integrative synthesis and meets APA 7th-edition style plus MARS/PRISMA/JARS reporting structure, including the ≤ 250-word abstract. Guides prose and structure; it does not run the analysis. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-writing-style) · ★1.1k)

- **psychological-safety**: Assesses and builds team psychological safety using Edmondson's framework and Clark's four developmental stages: inclusion, learner, contributor, and challenger safety. Identifies leadership behaviours that build or erode the shared belief that interpersonal risk-taking is safe. Use when team members withhold concerns, meetings lack candour, post-mortems reveal foreseeable failures that went unreported, or founders need to diagnose why innovation has narrowed. ([fatihguner/foreman](https://github.com/fatihguner/foreman/tree/HEAD/plugins/foreman/skills/psychological-safety) · ★52)
- **psychological-safety**: Build psychological safety in your team using Simon Sinek's Leaders Eat Last Circle of Safety model and Amy Edmondson's research. Use when a leader says "my team doesn't speak up", "people are afraid to fail", "nobody challenges bad ideas", "people only tell me what I want to hear", "I want my team to take more risks", "there's a blame culture", "people hide mistakes", or "I want to create a safe environment". Also trigger when someone describes a team where postmortems turn into blame sessions, where people stay quiet in meetings and then vent privately, where mistakes are hidden until they… ([qa-aman/claude-skills](https://github.com/qa-aman/claude-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/by-role/leadership/psychological-safety) · ★20)
- **psychological-safety**: Build and protect psychological safety on a design team. ([Owl-Listener/design-leadership-skills](https://github.com/Owl-Listener/design-leadership-skills/tree/HEAD/team-building/skills/psychological-safety) · ★14)
- **psychological-safety-practice**: Translates psychological safety theory into daily leader behaviours -- the specific words, reactions, and micro-actions that build or destroy a team's willingness to speak up. Covers misconceptions, responding to mistakes, inviting honest feedback, modelling healthy conflict, addressing bad behaviour immediately, and making mental health discussable. - **psychologist**: Applied psychology discipline for product, organisational, and research contexts. Covers the full analytical lifecycle: cognitive-bias diagnosis, behavioural experiment design, individual-differences modelling, group dynamics analysis, replication-crisis literacy, and ethical research conduct. Grounds every inference in evidence hierarchy (case study through meta-analysis) rather than pop-psychology shorthand. Distinct from `core/product-conversion-readiness` behavioural-nudge augmentation — this skill applies academic discipline to product decisions, not conversion optimisation tactics.
